- 16 games, 6 kickoff windows: Wed 9/9 (NE@SEA), Thu 9/10 (SF@LA **Melbourne**, neutral),
  Sun early ×8, Sun late ×4, SNF (DAL@NYG), MNF (DEN@KC).

  the polling). Weather: all outdoor games HORIZON until T-16d. Availability: ESPN ladder
  live (camp listings — designations will churn until cutdowns).

  loop before Week 1; nothing here is a real recommendation until the M8 ops cycle runs.

### Defect found and fixed this run (3rd gap, not one of the two under test)
`selftest.sh` check 17 asserted `len(live) == 2` — a hardcoded snapshot of the ledger's live-row
count. It went **red the moment this build logged its scan rows**, i.e. it would have failed on
the first real build every time, and doctrine says selftest-red = STOP. Replaced the count
assertion with structural ones that hold as the ledger grows: live rows must be ≥ the seed, must
never carry Bucket=BT, must all have a leg_id, and **leg_ids must be unique** (the invariant the
JOINs depend on, and the one that made the SEA ML de-dup above necessary). Selftest back to
**74/74 ✓** with the new scan rows in place.

## Run 2026-08-09 02:22Z — routine 4th firing (permissions + fold proof, post-`e9df90a`)
**Run type: BUILD** (detector: Saturday 22:21 ET, no imminent kickoffs). **T-31d** to the
Wed 2026-09-09 opener. Fired **~27 minutes after run 3** (01:56Z) — line movement is not
expected on that interval, and none was found.

### Preflight
- Selftest **74/74 ✓** (hook fired automatically; no manual recovery needed).
- Store synced; `*:2026` stats/snaps ABSENT (expected pre-season). Weekcheck W1 diff **clean**.
- PULSE: 0 decided legs — governor idles. **ODDS_MODE=rich.**

### Board
- `board reg 2026 1` — **3 cr**. Poll plan **1654 cr** for the week, inside `BUDGET_WEEKLY_SOFT=2500` ✓.
- **88 two-sided best-shopped pairs** across **10 books**, all devigged (run 3: 84 pairs).
  Overround **100.24% – 105.43%**; **zero sub-100% pairs** → no arb, no shopping edge.
  Scan math verified row-for-row against `devig.sh` (SF@LA 3.5 → 51.1/48.9; ATL@PIT ML →
  39.9/60.1; ARI@LAC ML → 17.8/82.2 — exact).
- **Line movement vs the ledger: zero.** 17 of the 49 logged W01 rows join a live pair by
  leg_id; every matched price is unchanged from run 3.

### Two corrections to the run-3 record
1. **Props ARE partially posted — run 3's "no prop markets posted" no longer holds.**
   Probing DEN@KC and DAL@NYG returned **`player_anytime_td`, DraftKings only, Yes side only**;
   the other 7 core markets returned 0 books (logged to `data/market_log.jsonl`). DAL@NYG was
   one of run 3's three claimed probes, and **`market_log.jsonl` carries no entries at 01:56Z**
   — its only timestamps are 2026-08-08T19:48 (1 line, DET@CIN) and this run's 02:22. Run 3's
   probe claim is therefore uncorroborated by the observation record.
2. **Prop probes are not free once a market has data.** Quota fell 19485 → 19483 across two
   probes: **1 credit each**, billed per market *returned*, not per market requested. The
   CLAUDE.md claim "empty-response prop calls bill 0 credits" holds only for genuinely empty
   responses — it does not generalise to "prop probes are free."

**No prop legs are constructible regardless**: a single book offering only the Yes side is
one-sided, so there is no no-vig baseline to devig against. `propquote.py` confirms:
`Yes -105 @DraftKings … 1-sided`.

### Tool fix — `propquote.py` on binary markets
`propquote.py` appended `_alternate` to every market, so `player_anytime_td` died on
`INVALID_MARKET — Invalid markets: player_anytime_td_alternate`, taking the standard market
down with it. Added `NO_ALTERNATE = {player_anytime_td, player_1st_td, player_last_td}`;
those now pull solo at 1 credit. Verified working against the live DK market above.

### `key_number_edge` — run 3's test was vacuous; this run performed it
Run 3 reported testing each book's 3.0/7.0 rung "against the half-point-below rung on the same
ladder … across every book and every game, zero hits." **That test cannot run on the featured
board**: all 143 book-events carry exactly **one** spread point each, so there is no second rung
to compare against — the comparison count is 0, not "zero hits."

Performed properly here by pulling **`alternate_spreads`** (real ladders, 11 games whose market
sits near a key number, **11 cr**, Bovada + BetRivers) and comparing each book's *implied mass
on the exact margin* to the **store's historical conditional** — `P(signed favourite margin = k)`
over 2015-2025 REG, bucketed by spread band (n=1211 for |spread| 2-4, n=624 for 6-8):

| benchmark | historical |
|---|---|
| favourite by exactly 3, spread 2-4 | 9.25% |
| dog by exactly 3, spread 2-4 | 7.02% |
| favourite by exactly 7, spread 2-4 | 5.12% |
| favourite by exactly 7, spread 6-8 | 6.09% |

**27 within-book key rungs tested. Every one lands within ±1.82pp of history; the largest
underpricing is +1.11pp (LV by 3, SEA by 3) — below the +2pp gate. `key_number_edge` does not
fire.** Two arithmetic errors were caught and fixed en route (comparing a one-directional book
mass against a two-directional historical figure, which manufactured 27 false hits; and a sign
flip on the dog side that produced negative probabilities) — the uniform 27/27 result was the
tell that the test, not the market, was wrong.

The rest of the registry is unchanged from run 3: weather adjustments HORIZON on all 12 outdoor
games, rest/short-week nil in Week 1, `intl_travel` symmetric at the MCG, `dome_pass_over`
declined, script/funnel/volume adjustments blocked on absent 2026 stats.

**Result: TrueP = market no-vig on all 88 legs → Edge +0.0pp everywhere.**

### Gates
- **Availability**: 16/16 ✓, ESPN live, 187 rows. **Patrick Mahomes (KC) QUESTIONABLE**
  (Surgery, ret 2026-08-15) and **Michael Penix Jr. (ATL) QUESTIONABLE** persist unchanged —
  every leg in **DEN@KC** and **ATL@PIT** stays **PENDING**. Standing: **Micah Parsons (GB) OUT**.
- **Weather**: 4 DOME + SF@LA at the MCG; the other 12 **HORIZON** (32-37d out). No number invented.

### Ledger
**Zero new rows — deliberately.** All 49 W01 rows already carry this run's leg_ids; dedup by
leg_id found no new bettable market. The 27 alternate-spread rungs were pulled to *test an
adjustment*, not as candidates, so logging them would manufacture calibration sample the run did
not earn. 49 W01 rows, 0 duplicate leg_ids — the JOIN invariant holds.

### Three tiers
`ticket.py` on the eight highest-floor legs (LAC -500 / JAX -360 / DET -325 / PHI -215 /
SEA -190 / LV -190 / LA -190 / CIN -185): **all eight dropped as thin** (vig-inclusive edges
-0.7 to -2.5pp) → `NO legs clear the bar → NO BET is the honest output.`

- **Tier 1 (best standalone): NO BET.** Nothing clears +2pp.
- **Tier 2 (highest floor 2-leg): NO BET.** No anchor clears +3-4pp.
- **Tier 3 (~+200 band): NO BET.** Floor cost is moot with an empty qualifying pool.

### Bankroll
**NO BET** — no devigged ≥+2pp favourite exists. Balance carries at **$10**, no row written.

### Provenance
- Board cache pulled 2026-08-09 02:21Z; premises re-snapshotted (16 games, 187 availability rows).
- **Odds API credits remaining: 19,468** (used 532 this month; 19,488 → 19,468). This run spent

  them wasted on the INVALID_MARKET call the fix above removes), `alternate_spreads` 12 (one

### Verdict
**Fourth consecutive T-30d+ build returning NO BET** across all three tiers and the bankroll —

checked against the observation record and did not survive, and the key-number test was then

## Run 2026-08-09 02:58Z — routine 5th firing (final notification protocol, post-`172deee`)
**Run type: BUILD** (detector: Saturday 22:52 ET, no imminent kickoffs). **T-31d** to the
Wed 2026-09-09 opener. Fired **~36 minutes after run 4** (02:22Z).

### Preflight
- Selftest **74/74 ✓** (hook fired automatically). Store synced; `*:2026` stats/snaps
  ABSENT (expected pre-season). Weekcheck W1 diff **clean** — premises stand.
- PULSE: 0 decided legs — governor idles. **ODDS_MODE=rich.**
- Git: all four `claude/admiring-johnson-*` tips already folded; clone base == `origin/main`
  @ `172deee`. Nothing to merge in.

### Board
- `board reg 2026 1` — **3 cr** (the run's entire spend).
- **89 two-sided best-shopped pairs** (36 spreads / 37 totals / 16 h2h) across **10 books**,
  all devigged. Overround **100.24% – 105.43%**; **zero sub-100% pairs** → no arb, no
  shopping edge. Devig math spot-checked against `devig.sh` (LA -3.5 −105/+104 → 51.1/48.9;
  ATL@PIT ML +150/−152 → 39.9/60.1 — exact).

### Line movement vs the ledger — the join, done properly
Run 4 reported "17 of the 49 logged W01 rows join a live pair by leg_id." That undercount was
a **join defect, not a market fact**: **all 49 rows join.** The failure mode was team-abbr
aliasing — `teams` maps *both* `LA` and `LAR` to "Los Angeles Rams", so a naive
name→abbr dict silently resolved to `LAR` while the ledger and `games` use `LA`, dropping
the three SF@LA rows; the other 29 were lost to pairing spreads on a shared `point`, when
the two sides of a spread rung carry **negated** points (home −3.5 / away +3.5). Keying the
pair on the home side's signed point recovers them. **49/49 joined, 0 duplicate leg_ids** —
the JOIN invariant holds.

**Two prices moved in 36 minutes, both immaterial:**

| leg | run 4 | now | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| NE@SEA Under 44.0 | −107 | −110 @MyBookie.ag | 3c worse |
| NO@DET Under 49.5 | −110 | −109 @Caesars | 1c better |

Both are juice ticks on a rung, not a number move; both legs remain **+0.0pp** edge because
TrueP is the no-vig of the same pair. No supersede rows written — a 1–3c tick on a NO-BET
candidate is not a changed recommendation, and logging it would inflate the calibration
sample with rows the run did not earn.

### `dome_pass_over` — declined by assertion in runs 3-4; **tested here**
The registry seeds `dome_pass_over +2pp` ("dome aids passing efficiency / totals Overs
(mild)"). Runs 3 and 4 declined it on the reasoning that the market already prices the roof.
That reasoning was never checked. Tested against the store — 2015-2025 REG, closing
`total_line` vs realised `total`, pushes excluded:

| sample | Over | Under | n | Over% | 95% CI | vs 50% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| fixed dome | 224 | 208 | 432 | 51.85% | [47.14, 56.56] | +1.85pp (+0.77 SE) |
| dome + retractable-closed | 412 | 404 | 816 | **50.49%** | [47.06, 53.92] | **+0.49pp (+0.28 SE)** |
| outdoors (control) | 965 | 1036 | 2001 | 48.23% | [46.04, 50.42] | −1.77pp (−1.59 SE) |

**`dome_pass_over` does not fire.** On the broadest indoor sample the Over rate is
+0.49pp — 0.28 SE, a CI straddling 50%, and far below the +2pp gate. Even the most
favourable slice (fixed-dome only) is +1.85pp at 0.77 SE and still under the gate. The
dome-vs-outdoors *gap* is real (+2.3pp) but it is the outdoor side that is below 50%, not
the indoor side above it — a dome Over is a coin flip, so the seed's sign is defensible and
its magnitude is not. **Recommend re-seeding it downward when calibration owns the registry;
it is not actionable at +2pp today.** Prior runs reached the right verdict for the wrong
reason.

Also corrected: run 4's gate note read "4 DOME + SF@LA at the MCG; the other 12 HORIZON"
(which totals 17 of 16 games). The MCG is **open-air** — `config/stadiums.csv` carries the
explicit override (`schedules row says dome — wrong; open-air`) and `weather.py` honours it,
so SF@LA is one of the 12 HORIZON rows. Correct split: **4 DOME, 12 HORIZON.** The static
table VETOing the feed's roof value is the doctrine working as designed.

### The rest of the registry
Unchanged and blocked for structural reasons, not oversight: all weather adjustments
(`wind_*`, `precip_under`, `cold_under`) **HORIZON** on all 12 outdoor games (32-37d out —
no number invented); `rest_edge`/`short_week_road` **nil** (every Week 1 game is 7/7 rest);

(that test is 36 minutes old and the board has not moved through a key number).

**Props: not re-probed, deliberately.** Run 4's observation record (`market_log.jsonl`,
45 entries, latest 02:26Z) establishes the current posting state — `player_anytime_td`,
DraftKings only, **Yes side only**; the other 7 core markets at 0 books. A one-sided market
has no no-vig baseline, so no prop leg is constructible regardless of price. Re-probing would
have spent credits to re-learn a 36-minute-old fact.

**Result: TrueP = market no-vig on all 89 legs → Edge +0.0pp everywhere.** This is
structural, not incidental: with TrueP derived from the pair it is tested against, the
vig-inclusive edge is exactly −(that side's share of the hold), which is ≤ 0 for every
pair on a board with no sub-100% rungs. Clearing +2pp requires either a named adjustment
firing or an arb. Neither exists today.

## Run 6 — BUILD (2026-08-09 15:40Z, scheduled)

**Verdict: NO BET** — sixth consecutive T-31d build with nothing clearing the gate. What this
run adds is a corrected scan and a measured retirement of `key_number_edge` at this range.

### Two scan bugs found and fixed this run
1. **Spread pairing.** The scan keyed two-sided rungs on `(market, point)`. For spreads that
   is wrong: the complement of `GB −1.5` is `MIN +1.5`, not `MIN −1.5` (two books disagreeing
   on who is favoured both post a `−1.5`, and pairing them devigs one side of the market
   against itself). Corrected pairing — `(teamX, +p)` against `(teamY, −p)` — takes the board
   from **59 rungs to 86**, i.e. the previous scans were blind to ~30% of the priced market.
   The verdict does not change (0 arbs before, 0 after), but the coverage claim in runs 1-5
   was overstated and the spread rungs they did report were mispaired.
2. **Push mass on the wrong side.** The first key-number pass used `P(|margin| = 3) = 14.65%`
   as the push mass for a `+3` dog. Wrong: the dog winning by 3 is a **win**, not a push. The
   correct quantity is `P(favourite wins by exactly 3)`, and conditioned on the line it is
   **9.25%** (112/1211, spreads 2.0-4.0, REG 2015-25) — a 5.4pp overstatement that manufactured
   five phantom "+2 to +5pp" edges.

### `key_number_edge` — measured, does not fire (0/12 rungs)
Six games carry a 3 or 7 rung. Corrected test (dog win = P(cover K−0.5); fav win = 1 − P(cover) − Q;
break-even = (1−Q)/(1+b)):

| Game | Rung | Best price | Model | Break-even | Edge |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| ATL@PIT | ATL +3 / PIT −3 | +100 / −105 | 48.07% / 42.68% | 45.38% / 46.48% | +2.70pp / −3.80pp |
| CHI@CAR | CAR +3 / CHI −3 | −110 / +100 | 48.94% / 41.81% | 47.54% / 45.38% | +1.41pp / −3.57pp |
| NO@DET | NO +7 / DET −7 | −105 / −105 | 48.26% / 45.65% | 48.10% / 48.10% | +0.16pp / −2.45pp |
| NYJ@TEN | NYJ +3 / TEN −3 | −120 / +100 | 49.35% / 41.40% | 49.50% / 45.38% | −0.15pp / −3.98pp |
| DAL@NYG | NYG +3 / DAL −3 | −115 / +100 | 48.54% / 42.22% | 48.54% / 45.38% | −0.01pp / −3.16pp |
| DEN@KC | DEN +3 / KC −3 | −114 / +103 | 48.67% / 42.08% | 48.34% / 44.71% | +0.33pp / −2.63pp |

The lone survivor, **ATL +3 at +2.70pp, is an artifact and was killed on inspection**:
**FanDuel is the only book at 2.5** — the other nine are all at PIT −3. So that "consensus"
baseline was one outlier book's half-point line compared against the eight-book price. Devigging
the pushable pair *itself* across those 8 books gives **P(ATL +3 | no push) = 49.48%** against a
best price of +100 (50.00%) → **−0.52pp**. The store agrees independently: at spreads 2.5-3.5
(n=1010) the dog covers +2.5 only **45.94%** of the time, *below* the 48.07% the artifact assumed.

**Doctrinal finding worth keeping:** devigging a *pushable* pair yields P(win | no push), and the
break-even for a pushable bet is exactly `1/(1+b)` in those same terms — the push mass cancels.
So whenever the market posts a two-sided price **at** the key number, `key_number_edge` is
structurally incapable of firing; it reduces to the ordinary vig test. It can only bite as a
half-point-buy question across rungs *within one book* — which this board prices efficiently.
That is the mechanism behind run 4's "27/27 ruled out", which had the right answer without the reason.

### Slate-wide scan — 86 rungs / 172 priced legs / 16 games
Best non-ML read per game (TrueP = best-shopped no-vig, `[adj: none]` throughout):

### Registry — nothing fires (run 6's two retirements independently re-tested)
- **`key_number_edge` — re-tested and re-killed on ATL@PIT.** The naive read (ATL +3 at +100
  against a "2.5 market") produces a tempting +1.4pp. It is the exact artifact run 6
  documented: the book-by-book board shows **FanDuel alone at 2.5**; the other nine books are
  all at PIT −3. Devigging the pushable pair *at* 3.0 gives P(ATL +3) = **49.40%** against a
  +100 best price (50.00% breakeven) → **−0.60pp**. Run 6's structural finding holds: when the
  market posts a two-sided price at the key number, the push mass cancels and
  `key_number_edge` reduces to the ordinary vig test.
- **`dome_pass_over`** — 4 true domes (NO@DET, ARI@LAC, MIA@LV, GB@MIN). Best case GB@MIN
  Over 45.0 at −107 (no-vig 50.0 → TrueP 52.0) pays a 51.69% breakeven → **+0.31pp**. Under gate.

- `script_*` / `funnel_*` / `blowout_snap_cap` blocked on absent 2026 snaps;
  `ol_mismatch_sacks` blocked on absent sack props; `market_disagrees` is negative-only.

**Doctrine note worth keeping.** `devig.sh` reports `Edge = TrueP − no-vig`, so a +2pp registry
adjustment mechanically reads "+2.0pp ✓ clears" there. `ticket.py` states the operative test in
its own header — *"edge = TrueP − price breakeven, vig INCLUDED; the no-vig gate ran upstream"* —
and that is the test runs 4-7 have applied. The two are complementary, not contradictory: an
adjustment must exceed 2pp **and** line-shopping must recover the vig. At this board the best
price still pays 0.1-1.5pp over the no-vig, so a flat +2pp seed cannot survive both.

### THE FINDING — `dome_pass_over` was being offered on an OPEN-AIR stadium (runs 9-12)
**SF@LA is played at the Melbourne Cricket Ground, which is open-air.** The nflverse
`schedules` row carries `roof='dome'` because a neutral-site row inherits the *home team's*
roof template (LA's). The code handles this correctly and always has: `config/stadiums.csv`
marks the MCG `outdoor` with an explicit note, `weather.py:is_dome()` short-circuits on
static-outdoor so the per-game 'dome' is vetoed, `weather.py week` prints SF@LA as
**HORIZON** (not DOME), and **`selftest.sh:330` asserts the veto directly**.

**Run 4 made this mistake, and run 5 caught and corrected it in writing** (build file
L428-429). Runs 6-8 were then correct ("4 true domes"). **Runs 9, 10, 11 and 12 all
regressed** — each wrote "4 true domes **+ the MCG neutral site**" and each listed
`SF@LA Over` inside the `dome_pass_over` candidate pool, which would have applied an
unearned **+2pp** to a leg played outdoors in Melbourne.

**Blast radius — bounded, and verified bounded, not assumed:**
- SF@LA Over was **never the best dome read** in any of those runs (run 12: +0.31pp vs
  NO@DET's +0.59pp), so it never reached a tier and never changed a verdict.
- **No ledger row carries the adjustment.** All 92 live rows are `[adj: none]`;
  `grep -c dome_pass_over ledgers/results_log.md` = **0**. The error never touched a priced leg.

So: a narrative-layer regression, contained by the fact that nothing was close to the gate.
But it re-appeared **four runs running after being explicitly corrected once**, and the
+2pp it would have applied is larger than the entire spread between the best and worst dome
candidate. That clears the process-lesson bar (2-3 sightings) with room to spare, and is
promoted to `CLAUDE.md` this run. **The tools were never wrong here — the prose was.** The
lesson is that a correction written only into a build file does not survive; it has to reach
doctrine.

## Run 14 — BUILD (2026-08-13 14:12Z, scheduled)

**Result: NO BET (all three tiers) + NO bankroll pick.** Ninth run running. T-27d to the Wed
opener. But the "no material change" line does **not** apply: the board printed its **first
zero-hold rung of the port**, the NO@DET total moved a full point, and five listed starters
changed designation — three of them on the same team.

### WHAT CHANGED vs Run 13 (2026-08-11 14:20Z)

| | Run 13 | Run 14 | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Best leg on the board | −0.12pp (SF@LA ±3.5) | **0.00pp (GB@MIN ML)** | **first non-negative rung of the port** — see below |
| Hold range | +0.24pp .. +5.43pp | **+0.00pp .. +5.43pp** | the floor of the range reached zero |
| Rungs / priced legs | 90 / 180 | **92 / 184** | +2 rungs, 0 unpaired |
| Legs clearing +2pp | 0 / 180 | **0 / 184** | ninth run running |
| Arbs | 0 | **0** | zero hold is not negative hold |
| NO@DET consensus total | **49.5** | **48.5** | −1.0 — the only line that moved a full point |
| Best dome read (NO@DET Over 49.5) | +0.59pp | **+0.81pp** | the best Under went −110 → −108, lifting the Over's no-vig 49.08% → 49.30% |
| Implied team totals | 32 values | **30 identical, 2 moved** | DET 28.25→27.75 · NO 21.25→20.75 |
| Newly-quoted rungs | 0 | **2** | first new rungs since run 12 |
| Availability rows | 174 | **171** | **5 newly-Q listed starters** |
| Credits spent | 3 | **6** | 3 featured board + 3 across four prop probes |

**THE BOARD'S FIRST ZERO-HOLD RUNG.** GB@MIN moneyline is quoted **GB +105 @Bovada / MIN
−105 @DraftKings**. Those two prices imply 48.780% + 51.220% = **100.000% exactly** —
`devig.sh -105 105` returns hold **0.0%**, and both sides devig to their own raw implied
price. Every previous run's best leg carried at least +0.24pp of hold; this is the first rung
on this board where shopping across books has removed the vig entirely.

It is worth being precise about what that is and is not. It is **not an arb** (hold is zero,
not negative) and it is **not a bet**: a zero-hold price means the market's own no-vig number
*is* the offered price, so the edge is **exactly 0.00pp** against a **+2pp** gate. What it
demonstrates is that the two-book shop is now tight enough to strip the hold off a pick'em —
which is the mechanism any future edge would have to come through. Logged, not bet.

to the end of the file, which placed them *after* `## Rollup` and therefore outside any counted

row(s) sit OUTSIDE any '## ' section`. They were moved into `## Recommended but NOT played`
and the count went 92 → 94. Appending to a sectioned ledger means appending to the *section*,
not the file.

## Run 15 — BUILD (2026-08-13 23:07Z, scheduled)

**Result: NO BET (all three tiers) + NO bankroll pick.** Tenth run running. T-27d to the Wed
opener. But this is emphatically **not** a "no material change" run: **the first player-prop
markets of the entire port have posted**, and the zero-hold rung run 14 found has already
closed.

### WHAT CHANGED vs Run 14 (2026-08-13 14:12Z, ~9h earlier)

| | Run 14 | Run 15 | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Player props** | none posted, 16/16 games | **POSTED on the 2 openers** | **first props of the port** — anytime-TD only, 2 books, **Yes side only** |
| Best leg on the board | 0.00pp (GB@MIN ML) | **−0.12pp (SF@LA ±3.5)** | **the zero-hold rung CLOSED** — DK moved MIN −105 → −118 |
| Hold range | +0.00pp .. +5.43pp | **+0.24pp .. +5.43pp** | floor back off zero |
| Rungs / priced legs | 92 / 184 | **90 / 180** | **−2 rungs**; 4 logged rungs now withdrawn board-wide |
| Legs clearing +2pp | 0 / 184 | **0 / 180** | tenth run running |
| Arbs | 0 | **0** | — |
| GB@MIN favourite | **GB** by 1.5 | **MIN** by 1.5 | schedules row **flipped sign** — now agrees with the market |
| Implied team totals | 32 values | **30 identical, 2 swapped** | GB 23.5→22.0 · MIN 22.0→23.5 |
| Newly-quoted rungs | 2 | **0** | nothing new to log; ledger holds at 94 rows |
| Availability rows | 171 | **171** | no designation changed in 9h |
| Credits spent | 6 | **12** | 3 featured + 9 prop-layer (see billing note) |

**THE ZERO-HOLD RUNG IS GONE — and it lasted about nine hours.** Run 14 logged the port's
first 0.00pp price: GB +105 @Bovada / MIN −105 @DraftKings, summing to exactly 100.000%.
DraftKings has since moved MIN to **−118**, and the best MIN price on the board is now
**−110 @BetRivers**. Best-shopped against GB +105 @Bovada that is a **+1.16pp** hold, so the
rung reverts to −0.56pp / −0.60pp per side. The board's best leg is once again **SF@LA ±3.5
at −0.12pp**, where it sat for runs 10-13. Nothing was bet on the zero-hold price and nothing
was lost by not betting it; what the episode actually demonstrates is that these prices are
**transient at the ~hours scale**, which is an argument for pricing at the lock, not early.

**GB@MIN: the schedules row flipped its favourite, and the market was right all along.**
`weekcheck diff` opened the run with a ⚠ — `spread MOVED -1.5 → 1.5 (≥1.5)`. Read carefully,
this is nflverse **correcting itself toward the market**, not the market moving:

| source | run 14 | run 15 |
|---|---|---|
| `games.spread_line` (nflverse) | −1.5 (**GB** favoured) | **+1.5 (MIN favoured)** |
| odds board consensus | MIN −1.5 / MIN −105 ML | MIN −1.5 / MIN −110 ML |
| `implied.py` team totals | GB 23.5 / MIN 22.0 | **GB 22.0 / MIN 23.5** |

At run 14 the schedules row and the market **disagreed about which team was favoured**; they
now agree. The ⚠ is therefore on the *schedules-derived* basis only — legs price off the odds
board, which did not move a full point. The two implied team totals are re-derived above and
are the only two values on the slate that changed.

**GB@MIN is also the most disagreed-upon spread on the board** — books are split across the
*sign*: BetRivers has **GB −1**, LowVig/BetOnline/Caesars have **pick'em**, DK/FD/Bovada/
MyBookie have **GB +1.5**. That 2.5-point spread of opinion opens a GB +1.5 (−110 @Bovada) /
MIN +1 (−114 @BetRivers) **middle**, which is worth naming only to kill it: it wins big on a
1-point MIN margin or a tie, loses $14 per $224 otherwise, and at realistic NFL 1-point/tie
frequencies (~4%) prices out around **−$7 per $224 = −3% EV**. Not a bet, and not logged as a
leg — the ledger prices sides, not middles.

### PLAYER PROPS HAVE POSTED — and correctly produce NO leg

The headline event of the run. `props <eid> core` on the two openers returns markets for the
first time since the port began:

| | NE@SEA | SF@LA |
|---|---|---|
| markets posted | `player_anytime_td` **only** | `player_anytime_td` **only** |
| markets still absent | the other **7** of the core-8 | the other **7** of the core-8 |
| books | DraftKings, Caesars | DraftKings, Caesars |
| sides quoted | **Yes only** | **Yes only** |
| players | DK 24 / Caesars 22 | DK 21 / Caesars 20 |

**Every quote is one-sided, so there is no devig, so there is no gate test.** The repo's own
tool says so rather than guessing — `propquote.py "Jaxon Smith-Njigba" player_anytime_td`
returns `1-sided` with both no-vig columns blank. Per doctrine a one-sided market's no-vig is
an **estimate** (raw − ~2.5pp), and an estimate cannot carry a +2pp gate. **No prop leg is
logged and no prop leg is proposed.** This is the correct output, not a shortfall.

What the boards *do* support is a cross-book disagreement read — raw implied only, sized to
tell the Friday designation run where to look. **This is not an edge and is not gate-tested:**

| game | player | best | other | raw gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SF@LA | **Christian Kirk** (SF **WR#3**, **QUESTIONABLE**) | +550 @DK | +340 @Caesars | **7.34pp** |
| NE@SEA | Emanuel Wilson | +325 @Caesars | +220 @DK | 7.72pp |
| NE@SEA | Rhamondre Stevenson | +175 @DK | +135 @Caesars | 6.19pp |
| SF@LA | Demarcus Robinson | +750 @DK | +475 @Caesars | 5.63pp |
| SF@LA | **Mike Evans** (SF **WR#1**, **QUESTIONABLE**) | +175 @DK | +145 @Caesars | 4.45pp |

**The pattern to watch: DraftKings is pricing the Questionable San Francisco pass-catchers
LONGER than Caesars** — Kirk +550 vs +340, Evans +175 vs +145 — which reads as DK having
priced the injury news and Caesars not yet having done so. That is exactly the stale-book
shape a real prop edge would come through, and it is **unverifiable while the No side is
missing**. It is the first thing the Friday designation run should re-probe.

**Coverage is also incomplete, which is not an edge either.** **Puka Nacua — the Rams' WR#1
by ID join against `depth_charts:2026` — is quoted at Caesars (+125) and absent from the
DraftKings board entirely.** Deebo Samuel and Khalil Herbert are DK-only; both NE and SEA
D/ST are DK-only. A player missing from one book is missing data, not a price.

**BILLING — a per-event prop call bills per market RETURNED, not per market requested.**
Verified this run: each `props <eid> core` call requested **8** markets, `data/market_log.jsonl`
recorded **7 with `books:0` and 1 with `books:2`**, and the quota moved by exactly **1 credit**
per call (18849 → 18847 across two calls plus two empty events; 18846 → 18842 across four).
This generalises the 2026-08-08 finding that empty prop calls bill 0. **It matters for the
budget**: `poll_scheduler.py plan` estimates **1408 cr/week** for props on the assumption of
8 markets × 176 event-polls. While only anytime-TD posts, actual props burn is **~1/8 of
that**, and it will rise market-by-market as the other seven post. The plan's number is a
correct *ceiling*, not a forecast — do not re-baseline runway on it yet.

### THE DESIGNATION BOARD — the actual news of the run

This is the first run where the injury board, not the market, carried the change. `availability
sync` wrote **117** ESPN listings (down from 126) over a **47**-row roster hard-OUT floor, with
**0** name-fallback joins. Diffed by `gsis_id` against the run-15 snapshot:

| | player | team | was | now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **cleared** | **Micah Parsons** | GB | **OUT** | not listed |
| **cleared** | **Jalen Ramsey** | PIT | **OUT** | not listed |
| cleared | Brenden Schooler | NE | OUT | not listed |
| cleared | Christian Izien | DET | OUT | not listed |
| cleared | Donte Kent | PIT | OUT | not listed |
| cleared | Jeremiah Webb | NE | IR | not listed |
| cleared | Mikail Kamara | SF | IR | not listed |
| cleared | James Conner | ARI | QUESTIONABLE | not listed |
| cleared | Derrick Moore | DET | QUESTIONABLE | not listed |
| cleared | Jimmy Rolder | DET | QUESTIONABLE | not listed |
| **added** | **Jordan James** | SF | — | **QUESTIONABLE** (Fracture) |

**Read this as "no longer listed", not as "confirmed active."** ESPN is best-effort by doctrine,
and a player dropping off the report is the normal way a designation clears — but it is the
*absence* of a listing, which is weaker evidence than a listing. It also bears saying plainly
that **these are August roster-status reports, not official game-week designations**: W1 kicks
off 2026-09-09, and the real Wednesday/Friday practice reports do not exist yet. A "Friday
designation run" 26 days out is a preseason-status update, and the availability ladder is
seeded directionally (Q=0.75, D=0.25, O/IR=0) precisely because these numbers are not yet earned.

**No leg was invalidated and nothing was superseded.** The `weekcheck diff` ⚠ (Jordan James) was
resolved by checking dependents: the ledger holds **zero player-market rows for 2026 W1** (the
only prop rows in the file are the 7 BT rows keyed `2025_10_*`), so no logged leg is premised on
any SF skill player. Every W1 row is h2h / spreads / totals, which the designation moves do not
touch at the leg level — the market prices the news, and TrueP is seeded from market no-vig.

**SF is now the most-compromised skill corps on the slate**: Jordan James (RB, Fracture), Kaelon
Black (RB), Christian Kirk (WR), De'Zhaun Stribling (WR), Mike Evans (WR) all QUESTIONABLE at
P(plays)=0.75, plus George Kittle OUT (Surgery) and Ricky Pearsall IR. Twelve SF listings against
two for the Rams. That is the game to watch when props actually post both sides.

### The prop re-probe run 15 asked for — and what it actually showed

Run 15 flagged DraftKings pricing the Questionable SF pass-catchers **longer** than Caesars and
called it "exactly the stale-book shape a real prop edge would come through", to be re-probed
today. Re-probed. **The hypothesis did not survive.**

| game | player | run 15 DK | run 16 DK | Caesars | gap then | gap now |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SF@LA | **Christian Kirk** (Q) | +550 | **+550** | +340 | 7.34pp | **7.34pp** |
| SF@LA | **Mike Evans** (Q) | +175 | **+170** | +145 | 4.45pp | **3.78pp** |
| SF@LA | Demarcus Robinson | +750 | **+600** | +475 | 5.63pp | **3.11pp** |
| NE@SEA | Rhamondre Stevenson | +175 | **+160** | +135 | 6.19pp | **4.09pp** |
| NE@SEA | Emanuel Wilson | +220 | **+225** | +325 | 7.72pp | **7.24pp** |

If run 15's reading were right — DK has the injury news, Caesars is stale — **Caesars** should
have moved toward DK. Instead **DraftKings did all the moving**, shortening three of five toward
Caesars while Caesars held. That is the ordinary shape of an early board tightening as it
matures, not a stale book about to be picked off. The honest conclusion is that the gaps were
**DK's opening width**, and the "edge" was an artefact of reading a one-sided board.

The one item that stays live: **Christian Kirk's 7.34pp gap did not move at all** while every
other gap closed, and Kirk is still QUESTIONABLE with no clarity. That is worth re-probing again
— as an observation, not a position.

**None of this is bettable and none of it is logged.** All quotes remain **Yes-side only**, so
there is no devig, so there is no no-vig baseline, so there is no gate test. `propquote.py`
returns `1-sided` with both no-vig columns blank. A one-sided market's no-vig is an *estimate*,
and an estimate cannot carry a +2pp gate. **0 prop legs proposed, 0 logged.** Correct output.

row came last and minted `2026_01_SF_LAR` — a game_id that exists nowhere. The canonical id is
`2026_01_SF_LA`, straight from `games`, and the ledger had it right all along. **Resolve game_ids
from the `games` table, never by rebuilding them from a name→abbr map** — the store deliberately
carries alias rows, and a name-keyed map silently picks one. No file was written from the bad
map; caught before the ledger was touched.

### THE NEWS — the first two-sided props, and why the gate clearing is a trap

`props <NE@SEA> core` returned **four** markets where every prior run saw one:

| market | quote (Fanatics — **sole** book) | hold | no-vig |
|---|---|---|---|
| Drake Maye **pass yds** | Over 215.5 **−120** / Under **−110** | **6.9pp** | Over 51.0% |
| Jaxon Smith-Njigba **rec yds** | Over 90.5 **−110** / Under **−120** | **6.9pp** | Over 49.0% |
| Sam Darnold **rush yds** | Over 5.5 **−140** / Under **+105** | **7.1pp** | Over 54.5% |

Two-sided at last, so for the first time this season a prop has a **devig, a no-vig baseline, and
therefore a gate test**. One registered adjustment fires: **NE is a +3.5 dog**, so
`script_pass_dog +3pp` applies to **Drake Maye Over 215.5**:

```
baseline (Fanatics no-vig)   51.0%
  +3.0pp  script_pass_dog
TrueP                        54.0%        [adj: script_pass_dog+3]
edge vs no-vig baseline      +3.0pp   ← CLEARS the +2pp standalone gate
```

**And it is still NO BET.** The price you can actually buy is **−120**, whose breakeven is
**54.55%**. TrueP 54.0% − 54.55% = **−0.55pp**: the leg loses money at the offered price even if
the adjustment is exactly right. The **6.9pp hold eats the entire modelled edge** — for scale,
the featured board's tightest rung this week holds **0.24pp**, i.e. **29× tighter**.

That is the lesson, and it is a general one: **the +2pp no-vig gate assumes a best-SHOPPED price.
With one book there is no shopping, and the no-vig baseline stops being a market consensus — it
becomes that book's own opening midpoint. Clearing the gate against a single 7pp-hold quote is
not evidence of edge; it is evidence of width.** `ticket.py` reaches the same verdict
independently by its own route, dropping the leg as `✗ thin` on vig-included edge.

The other two: **JSN** is on the **favourite**, so `script_pass_dog` does not apply and no other
registered adjustment fits — `[adj: none]`, edge +0.0. **Darnold rush yds** gets nothing either;
`script_rush_fav` is an **RB** rule and stretching it onto a QB scramble line would be inventing
registry, not applying it. All three are logged as scan rows flagged `⚠1-BOOK BASELINE`.

### The prior-season base rates — a flag, deliberately NOT a bet

Because the store finally had something to say about a posted prop, the lines were checked
against 2025 REG production (`player_week`, ID-joined):

| leg | line | 2025 avg | games over line | Fanatics no-vig |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Maye pass yds | 215.5 | **258.5** | **14 / 17 (82%)** | 51.0% |
| JSN rec yds | 90.5 | **105.5** | **13 / 17 (76%)** | 49.0% |
| Darnold rush yds | 5.5 | 5.6 | **6 / 17 (35%)** | **54.5%** |

Read at face value these are gigantic gaps — and **none of them is bettable, by design.** A raw
prior-season hit rate is **not a TrueP**: doctrine requires TrueP to be a market no-vig baseline
plus *pre-registered named* adjustments, and there is no registered "last season's over rate"
adjustment. There is also a real methodological hole — applying **this** week's fixed line
retroactively to a season in which the line moved with form overstates the rate, and a Week-1
opener carries roster and scheme change the store cannot see. Minting an adjustment to capture
these numbers would be manufacturing doctrine the ledger has not earned. **Logged as an
observation to re-test when a second book posts; zero legs proposed.**

The Darnold line is the one to watch: it is the only one where the book sits on the *opposite*
side of history (35% historical vs 54.5% no-vig priced), i.e. the **Under** is the interesting
side. If a second book posts and the consensus still prices Over ~54%, that is worth a real look.

### ⚠ NEW PROCESS OBSERVATION (sighting 1 of 2-3 — not yet doctrine)

**Fanatics — and only Fanatics — quotes anytime-TD on players ESPN lists OUT (Surgery).** Checked
by joining every prop quote against the `availability` table:

| game | book | player | quote | store designation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SF@LA | **Fanatics** | **George Kittle** | +250 | **OUT — Surgery**, P(plays)=0.0 |
| NE@SEA | **Fanatics** | **Zach Charbonnet** | +110 | **OUT — Surgery**, P(plays)=0.0 |

DraftKings and Caesars quote **neither** player. Charbonnet's +110 is nearly the shortest RB price
on that board. This matters directly: **Fanatics is the sole source of all three two-sided markets
above**, so the one book supplying the season's first devigable prop baseline is the same book
demonstrably not pricing availability. That is a second, independent reason its no-vig is not a
market consensus.

**Candidate rule, pending confirmation:** *before using a new book's prop board as a devig
baseline, cross-check its quotes against the `availability` table; a book quoting P(plays)=0
players is not injury-aware and its no-vig is an opening midpoint, not a consensus.* Two instances
in one run is **one sighting**, not two — CLAUDE.md wants 2-3 sightings across runs before a
process rule is promoted. Re-check next run; if Fanatics still quotes OUT players, promote it.

**Tooling note — the ledger's leg_id lives at cell index 3, not 2.** A first pass at the
rung-coverage diff split rows on `|` and read `cells[2]`, which is the **Leg label**, so every one
of the 186 board leg_ids came back "newly quoted" and 0 came back withdrawn — a result absurd
enough to catch itself. `legs.COL["leg_id"]` is the answer (the leading empty cell from the
row's opening pipe shifts every index by one). **Use `legs.COL`, never a hand-counted index** —
the same class of bug as the run-16 team-abbr map, and the same fix: ask the codec.

against. Anytime TD is not a two-way market — the field is many players deep — so a "No" side

integrity alarm. The pipeline's canonical abbreviation is `LA` (what `games`, the slate and
every ledger row use). Re-keyed the lookup to the abbreviations actually present in this
week's `games` rows and the phantom diff vanished. **No ledger row was written from the bad
pass**, and no leg_id integrity problem exists. Worth recording because the failure mode is
generic: joining on `teams` without constraining to the season under scan will silently pick
up relocated-franchise aliases.

### THE FINDING — 75 of 79 QUESTIONABLE rows carry a return date that has already lapsed

Runs 19 and 20 each carried a watch item: *"Patrick Mahomes' listed return date (2026-08-15)
has now LAPSED and ESPN still lists him QUESTIONABLE."* Tracked as a Mahomes anomaly, it
looked like one team's stalled update. It is not. Querying the whole gate:

```
QUESTIONABLE rows, 2026 W1                                     79
  ... whose listed ret_date is already in the past (< 2026-08-18)   75
```

**95% of the Q tier is running on a lapsed listing.** The dates cluster on 2026-08-13 /
08-14 / 08-15 — training-camp report dates, not game-week designations. Only four Q rows
carry a date still ahead: Michael Penix Jr. (ATL, 08-28), Darren Waller (CAR, 09-13),
Zach Charbonnet (SEA, 09-09, and he is OUT not Q), George Kittle (SF, 09-10 — kickoff day).

Why this matters, stated precisely and without overclaiming:

- `availability.py` seeds **P(plays)=0.75 for every Q**. That seed is directional and
  calibration is meant to own it. But it is currently being applied to 79 players of whom
  75 hold a designation whose own stated expiry has passed — i.e. **the listing is a camp
  artifact, and the 0.75 is being read off a field that has stopped being refreshed**, not
  off a game-week injury report.
- **No leg is affected.** Nothing on the board is within 0.6pp of the +2pp gate, all 106
  ledger rows are `[adj: none]`, and no availability-derived haircut has ever been applied.
  This is a finding about what the gate will be worth in three weeks, not a live error.
- **It is not a code bug.** The tool records what ESPN publishes, flags nothing it did not
  see, and the gate table reads ✓ on all 16 games because no *hard* OUT touches a priced leg.
  The lapse is upstream and real; what was missing was anyone measuring its extent.

**What this changes for the Friday designation run.** Designation haircuts are the one place
the Q tier feeds a number. Taking a 0.75 off a lapsed camp listing would put a haircut on a
player who may well be fully cleared — an invented adjustment in the same family as the
`dome_pass_over`-on-an-open-air-stadium error, just pointing the other way. **The right move
is to treat a Q whose ret_date has lapsed as UNVERIFIED, not as 0.75**, and to say so rather
than silently haircut. Registering this as a candidate rule rather than promoting it now:
**sighting 1 of 2**. Pre-registered promotion condition, in the run-17→18 pattern: *if the
lapsed-Q share is still above ~50% at the Friday designation run (2026-08-21), promote to
CLAUDE.md as an availability rule.* If real injury reports post in game week and the share
collapses, the rule was never needed and does not get written.

### THE SECOND FINDING — a stale rung is not a dead rung

Run 20 reported CLE@JAX 7.0 as having "lasted exactly one run" and wrote that **"that cross
is retired."** Two rungs it listed as stale in the same breath are **back on the board this
run**:

| rung | run 20 | run 21 |
|---|---|---|
| CHI@CAR spreads 3.0 | listed stale | **live** (game now quotes 2.5 + 3.0) |
| NO@DET totals 48.5 | listed stale | **live** (game now quotes 48.5 / 49 / 49.5) |
| CLE@JAX spreads 7.0 | died | still gone (CLE@JAX remains single-rung at 7.5) |

Three weeks out, books add and drop rungs freely; a rung absent from one 15-hour snapshot
has not been *retired*, it has been *absent once*. Run 20's wording would have had a later
run treat the CLE@JAX key-7 cross as permanently closed. **Read "stale" as "not quoted in
this snapshot"**, and re-test a cross each run rather than carrying forward a retirement.
This is a prose-discipline lesson of exactly the kind the venue-type rule already covers, so
it is recorded here as **sighting 1** and not promoted; the doctrine bar is 2-3 sightings.

### THE THIRD FINDING — the run-18 Fanatics rule survives its first contrast test

Fanatics has now been off the entire W1 board for two runs, so there is **no sighting 4** and
none is claimed; the rule stands at 3. But the two books that remain were run through the
same cross-check, which is the first time the rule's discriminator has been pointed at a
book that ought to pass:

| book | events | market | P(plays)=0 players quoted |
|---|---|---|---|
| DraftKings | NE@SEA, SF@LA | `player_anytime_td` | **0** |
| Caesars | NE@SEA, SF@LA | `player_anytime_td` | **0** |

Kittle (SF, OUT), Pearsall (SF, IR), Charbonnet (SEA, OUT) and Emmanwori (SEA, OUT) appear on
neither board, while Fanatics quoted Kittle at +250 and Charbonnet at +110 as recently as
run 18. **The test discriminates**: it does not simply flag every book. That strengthens the
existing rule without adding a sighting to it.

### THE FIX — a franchise-alias bug was silently hiding two teams' listings for the entire port

Run 20 caught a scan-script pass that reported every SF@LA row as a fresh un-logged game and
correctly identified the cause: the `teams` table carries **both** `LA` and `LAR` rows for
"Los Angeles Rams", so a plain `team_name → team_abbr` dict kept whichever came last. Run
20's fix was tactical — it re-keyed **that one scan's** lookup to the abbreviations in the
week's `games` rows. That fix worked for the scan, and the run correctly wrote its own
lesson: *"joining on `teams` without constraining to the season under scan will silently
pick up relocated-franchise aliases."*

The lesson survived in prose. The **same bug** in `availability.py sync` did not survive
inspection because nobody looked. Verified this run:

```
before the fix:  ARI=12  AZ=1   LA=2   LAR=8
after the fix:   ARI=13         LA=10
```

**Ten Rams listings and one Cardinals listing were being filed under abbreviations that
neither the gate table, the slate, nor any leg_id ever looks up.** The gate row `SF @ LA
listed SF:15 LA:2` — reported unchanged for five consecutive runs — was reading a
two-player LA table while eight Rams listings sat in an `LAR` bucket. The hidden Rams
listings include:

| player | designation | detail | ret |
|---|---|---|---|
| Puka Nacua (WR) | QUESTIONABLE | Soreness | 2026-08-22 |
| Myles Garrett (DE) | QUESTIONABLE | Not Specified | 2026-08-22 |
| Alaric Jackson (OT) | QUESTIONABLE | Not Specified | 2026-08-22 |
| Davis Allen (TE) | QUESTIONABLE | Not Specified | 2026-08-22 |
| Justin Dedich (G) | **OUT** | Not Specified | 2026-08-22 |
| Keagen Trost (OT) | QUESTIONABLE | Not Specified | 2026-09-10 (kickoff-day) |
| Eddie Walls III (LB) | IR | Not Specified | 2027-02-15 |
| Mario Williams (WR) | IR | Not Specified | 2027-02-15 |

**Why this matters, stated precisely and without overclaiming:**

- **No leg is affected retroactively.** All 108 ledger rows are `[adj: none]`, no board has
  cleared the +2pp gate on a Rams read, and no availability-derived haircut has ever fired
  in the port's history. The bug was invisible because the gate never armed.
- **The run-18 earned rule "cross-check a prop book against `availability` before using it
  as a devig baseline" was PROMOTED on a Rams-adjacent finding — Kittle (SF) and Charbonnet
  (SEA), both P(plays)=0.** The rule itself does not depend on the Rams listings, and the
  test still discriminates (Fanatics quoted OUT players, DK/Caesars did not). But its
  evidence base was the SF@LA event, and this run finds the SF row of that event has been
  reading correctly (15 listings) while the LA row has been reading a phantom (2 listings)
  the whole time. **The rule stands, but the sample it was promoted on was smaller and
  weaker than the ledger showed** — a 10-player LA board would have made the discrimination
  stronger, not weaker, so the promotion direction is unchanged.
- **The run-21 lapsed-Q finding does not survive the resync.** Run 21 measured "75 of 79
  QUESTIONABLE rows carry a ret_date already in the past" and registered it as sighting 1
  of 2, with a pre-registered condition of re-check at the Fri designation run. After the
  fix + resync: **0 of 193 Q rows are lapsed**; every ret_date is 2026-08-20 or later.
  The lapsed field measured 48 hours ago was a stale snapshot ESPN has since refreshed; it
  is not a systemic pattern, and the candidate rule **retires at sighting 0**, not 1. This
  is exactly what the "register as candidate, promote only on repeat sighting" doctrine
  exists to catch — a first sighting that does not replicate never becomes a rule.
- **A run-21 line that read "the KC read still rests on Mahomes, who is one of the 75
  lapsed-Q rows" is corrected: Mahomes' ret_date reads 2026-08-22, two days ahead, in the
  fresh sync.** The PENDING flag stays because Mahomes is still Q and 2026-08-22 is still
  training-camp report timing rather than a game-week designation, but not because his date
  has lapsed.

**The fix, in the code**: `tools/availability.py` now derives `canon(abbr)` from the
abbreviations this season's `games` rows actually use, and (a) canonicalises the roster
floor's `team`, (b) canonicalises the ESPN feed's team-name → abbr resolution, preferring
the abbreviation `games` uses when a name maps to more than one, (c) canonicalises the
player-name bridge's `latest_team` key. Aliases are applied ONLY when their target is a
live abbreviation — a stale alias can never invent a team. Selftest still passes **97/97**.
Not adding a new selftest case here yet: the doctrine bar for a new assertion is two
sightings of the same failure class, and this is sighting 2 of the venue-type/franchise-alias
pattern (run 13's `dome_pass_over` on SF@LA was sighting 1). If a third alias-shaped read
lands wrong, promote to a selftest assertion at that point.

### THE FREEZE BROKE — two totals moved a full point after six runs of nothing

Runs 18–22 produced **32 identical implied team totals across five consecutive runs** while
the quotes around them churned. That ended this run. Two games repriced, both by a full
point of total, both with the spread untouched:

| game | run 22 total | run 23 total | spread | implied then → now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ATL @ PIT | 41.5 | **42.5** | PIT −3 (unchanged) | PIT 22.25→**22.75** · ATL 19.25→**19.75** |
| CHI @ CAR | 46.5 | **47.5** | CHI −2.5 (unchanged) | CHI 24.5→**25.0** · CAR 22.0→**22.5** |

Stated precisely: **the spread did not move in either game, so this is not a change of
opinion about who wins — it is a change of opinion about pace.** Both moves are +1.0, both
lift each side by exactly +0.5, and both are recorded in the nflverse `schedules` sync
(source stamp `Thu, 20 Aug 2026 22:36:39 GMT`), not inferred from the odds cache. The
board's own rungs corroborate: ATL@PIT is now quoted 41.5 / 42.0 / 42.5 and CHI@CAR
47.5 / 48.0, with the older Under 45.5 / 46 / 46.5 / 47 rungs the ledger carries from runs
13–19 all dead.

`weekcheck.py diff` correctly reported **no material line move** — its total threshold is
**≥2.0** and these are +1.0 — so this is the gate behaving as specified, not missing
something. Worth recording rather than acting on: a one-point total move on a 42.5 game is
real information about pace, but it moves no leg through a +2pp gate, and **`dome_pass_over`
cannot be applied to either game** — ATL@PIT is outdoors (HORIZON, 24d out) and CHI@CAR is
outdoors (Bank of America Stadium). No adjustment fires.

The five NE@SEA prop leg_ids run 22 logged (Maye O215.5/O235.5 pass yds, Doubs O30.5 and
A.J. Brown O90.5 rec yds, Henderson O5.5 rush yds) are now **stale rungs** — carried, not
retired, per the run-21 finding that "stale" ≠ "retired". This is the second full
appearance-then-withdrawal cycle of the W1 prop board (run 18 → run 19, run 22 → run 23),
which is worth naming as a **candidate pattern, not a rule**: *a prop board that posts
20+ days out is provisional and may be pulled within hours; do not treat a single sighting
of two-sided props as the market having opened.* Sighting 2 of 2 on the observation, but
**the doctrine bar is a rule that changes an action** and this one does not yet — registering
it as a candidate and re-checking at the Friday designation run.

**A parser note worth keeping:** the pick'em rungs (BUF@HOU and GB@MIN at 0) are logged in
the ledger as `spreads:HOU:-0:` and `spreads:MIN:-0:`. A naive `f"{point:+g}"` renders the
same rung as `+0`, which makes an already-logged rung look brand new. This run caught it
before writing and logged **0** new rungs rather than 2 duplicates. The general form is the
one `legs.py` exists to prevent: **`-0` and `+0` are the same number but not the same
string, so any leg_id built by formatting rather than by the codec can silently fork a
rung.** Registering as sighting 1 of the zero-rung/leg_id-formatting class; if it recurs,
promote to a selftest assertion.

availability drops — premises stand"* while the very same command, re-run after §5 had
written 293 fresh ESPN listings, **exits 1 with 15 findings**. Neither reading is wrong —
they are diffs against different store states — but **the digest's §4 line is a pre-sync
reading and must not be taken as the run's pre-lock verdict.** The run has to re-run
`weekcheck diff` after the sync, which is what produced the table above. Registering as
sighting 1 of the stale-digest-gate class; if a second run has to make the same correction,
it belongs in doctrine (or §4 should move below §5).

### THE PROP BOARD DID NOT WITHDRAW — IT EXPANDED. And the way this run nearly
### reported the opposite is the most important thing in it.

**What is actually on the board.** Two events carry a **full two-sided prop board across
four books**:

| event | books | markets | outcomes | two-sided rungs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **NE@SEA** | DraftKings · FanDuel · Caesars · BetRivers | 13 | 145 | — |
| **SF@LA** | DraftKings · Caesars · FanDuel · BetRivers | 13 | 125 | — |
| **DEN@KC · DAL@NYG** | DraftKings only | anytime-TD only | — | 0 (one-sided) |
| **ATL@PIT · NO@DET · WAS@PHI · BAL@IND** | none | — | — | 0 |

Across the two live boards: **50 two-sided rungs / 100 priced prop legs**, in
`player_pass_yds`, `player_pass_tds`, `player_rush_yds`, `player_receptions` and
`player_reception_yds`. Against run 22's single-book 22-rung board and run 23's zero, this
is **the largest prop board of the port by a wide margin** — and it is four books deep, so
it is no longer a one-book baseline.

**THE PROCESS FAILURE, recorded in full because it nearly went out in the email.** This run
first probed all seven events with `tools/odds_api.sh props <eid> keys` and got back, seven
times:

```
  (no prop markets posted for this event yet — normal outside game week / preseason)
```

and wrote that up as a complete withdrawal of the prop board. **Every one of those seven
calls was an API error.** `keys` is documented in CLAUDE.md as a `props` alias but was
**never implemented in `cmd_props`** — the case statement handles `core|kicking|defense|
longest` only — so `keys` went to The Odds API as a literal market name and came back:

```json
{"message":"Invalid markets: keys","error_code":"INVALID_MARKET"}
```

The renderer then did the damage. It branches on `(.bookmakers | length) == 0`, and on an
error payload `.bookmakers` is null, so `null | length` is 0 and the tool printed the
reassuring pre-posting message. **A hard failure was rendered as a benign, expected,
doctrine-blessed absence** — the one disguise a failure must never be allowed to wear here,
because "no props posted yet" is a state this system sees constantly and correctly ignores.
The check that caught it was cheap and should have come first: the quota did not move, which
was read as "empty probes are free" (true, and documented) when it equally meant "these calls
did nothing".

Both defects are fixed this run:

1. **`tools/odds_api.sh` — `keys` is implemented** as the 1-credit single-market probe it was
   always documented to be (`PROPS_CORE`'s first market), so the alias no longer falls
   through as a literal.
2. **`tools/odds_api.sh` — an API error now fails loudly and returns 1**, before the
   observation log is written, printing `⛔ PROP CALL FAILED — this is an ERROR, not an
   absence.` A failed call can no longer print the pre-posting message *or* write a
   `books:0` row into `data/market_log.jsonl` — a false absence in the committed observation
   record would have corrupted the posting-time history this ledger is accumulating.
3. **`tools/selftest.sh` §2b — three new assertions**: every alias documented in CLAUDE.md
   (`core|kicking|defense|longest|keys`) resolves in the case statement; the error guard
   exists; and it precedes the market_log write. **Verified to FAIL on injected drift** —
   deleting the `keys` branch turns the check red with *"unimplemented alias falls through as
   a literal market"*. Selftest is now **100/100**, up from 97.

The general lesson, and the reason it is written here rather than only in the fix: **an error
that renders as an expected state is invisible, and "the quota did not move" means a call
was free OR that it never happened.** The absence probe is the one call in this system whose
success and whose failure look identical, so it is the one that had to be made loud.

**Cost of the correction: ~34 credits** (17,986 → 17,952) for the seven real `core` probes
that established the truth, plus the wasted-but-free invalid calls.

### The run-18 earned rule got its sharpest test yet — and needs a refinement, not a rewrite

The rule: **cross-check a prop book against `availability` before using it as a devig
baseline.** Run 18 promoted it when *Fanatics alone* quoted OUT players while DraftKings and
Caesars quoted neither — the test named one bad book. Joining all 51 quoted players on this
run's boards against `availability`:

**Exactly one quoted player is listed P(plays) ≤ 0.3: George Kittle (SF, TE, OUT/Surgery,
P(plays)=0.0) — and he is quoted by DraftKings +215, Caesars +215 AND FanDuel +210.**

So the discriminator no longer discriminates: **three major books fail it simultaneously.**
That admits two readings, and the honest one is not the flattering one:

- *(a) all three books are not injury-aware 20 days out* — consistent with the rule as
  written, or
- *(b) the store is behind the market.* **Kittle's `ret_date` is 2026-09-10, which is SF@LA's
  kickoff day itself.** A book pricing him at ~32% to score is not obviously wrong; the
  store's P(plays)=0 comes from a designation his own return date contradicts.

**Reading (b) is the live one**, and it means the run-18 rule's test detects *disagreement
between the store and the book*, which is not the same thing as *the book being sloppy* —
the direction of the error is not established by the test itself. That is a refinement to an
earned rule on **one sighting**, so it is **not** being written into CLAUDE.md. Pre-registering
the condition: **if a second occasion arises where every book on a board quotes a store-OUT
player whose `ret_date` falls on or before kickoff, the rule gets a clause — a P(plays)=0
whose return date has already arrived is a CONTESTED availability, not a book error, and the
flag belongs on the store row rather than the baseline.**

**What this does NOT change for this run's pricing**, and the precision matters: Kittle
appears **only in `player_anytime_td`, which is quoted one-sided (Yes only) and is therefore
never devigged.** He contaminates no two-sided baseline. The 50 devigable rungs come from
four books, so **no `⚠1-BOOK BASELINE` flag is owed** — the run-22 condition that produced it
(a single book being the sole source of every two-sided prop) does not hold any more.

**One unresolved join, flagged not guessed:** `Deebo Samuel` is quoted on the SF@LA board but
has **no 2026 roster row** in the store (`rosters` season=2026), so his team could not be
resolved by ID join and he was excluded from the dog-roster filter below rather than assigned
a team from memory. 49 of 51 quoted players resolved; the other exclusions are team D/ST
entries, which are not players.

### Props scored — 0 of 100 clear the gate, and the holds say why

| | value |
|---|---|
| Two-sided prop rungs | **50** (100 priced legs) |

**0 of 15 reach +2pp**, and the best is +0.75pp *with a +3pp adjustment already granted*.
The reason is the hold: **every prop rung on this board holds 5.95–7.19pp**, against
0.77–4.98pp on the featured board. A ~6pp hold eats a 3pp adjustment and most of the
baseline before the gate is even reached. This is exactly the WIDTH-not-edge reading the
run-18 rule warns about, arrived at from the other direction: the board is four books deep
and still unbettable, because four books quoting a wide number is not the same as a market.

with 41 unchanged. That is the largest reprice of the port, and it lines up with BetMGM
leaving the board — the book of record changed on many rungs whether or not the number did.

Stated precisely: **a lot of the board moved, and none of it moved enough to change a
verdict.** `weekcheck diff` reports no material line move, correctly — its spread threshold
is ≥1.5 and its total threshold ≥2.0, and the largest move here is a *no-vig* shift of
1.08pp on an unchanged 47.5 number. The 15 findings that make it exit 1 are all availability.

### Codec — the `-0` / `+0` leg_id fork is FIXED (sighting 2, promotion condition met)

Run 23 caught this by hand and pre-registered it: *"registering as sighting 1 of the
zero-rung/leg_id-formatting class; if it recurs, promote to a selftest assertion."*
**It recurred this run** — GB@MIN is quoted pick'em, and `format_leg_id(..., 0.0)` renders
`spreads:MIN:+0:` while the ledger has carried `spreads:MIN:-0:` since run 13. Same rung,
two strings; a rung already in the ledger reads brand new.

The cause is in the codec itself, not in a caller: `f"{point:+g}"` formats `0.0` as `+0` and
`-0.0` as `-0`, so **which of the two you get depends on a signed zero nobody is tracking**.
Two changes:

1. **`tools/legs.py` — `format_leg_id` normalises the pick'em spread to the single canonical
   `-0`**, which is what the ledger already carries (`BUF_HOU:spreads:HOU:-0:` and
   `GB_MIN:spreads:MIN:-0:`, 2 rows each). Non-spread markets are unaffected and stay unsigned.
2. **`tools/selftest.sh` §16 — an assertion pins it**: all three of `0.0`, `-0.0` and `0`
   must produce one leg_id, that leg_id must be `-0`, it must parse back to `point == 0`, and
   a totals 0 must still render unsigned. **Verified to FAIL on injected drift** — reverting
   the codec line turns the check red with `pick'em leg_id forked on signed zero`, and
   restoring it returns **97/97**.

This is the run-13 lesson applied to code rather than prose: a correction that lives only in
a build file regresses. Run 23 wrote the correct diagnosis into `builds/`, and the same fork
was waiting in the codec 22 hours later.

### DOCTRINE: two pre-registered conditions closed out this run

**1. The stale-digest gate — sighting 2, FIXED IN CODE.** Run 24 registered sighting 1 and
wrote the promotion condition: *"if a second run has to make the same correction, it belongs
in doctrine (or §4 should move below §5)."* This run made exactly the same correction — the
digest printed **"✓ no QB changes, no availability drops — premises stand"** and the identical
command, re-run after the sync, **exited 1 with 43 findings including a starting QB.**

Taking the parenthetical, because it removes the class rather than documenting it:
**`session_start.sh` §4 and §5 are swapped** — the availability sync now runs BEFORE the
weekcheck diff, so the digest's gate line is a post-sync verdict. A comment on the blocks
says the order is load-bearing, `selftest.sh` asserts `availability.py sync` precedes
`weekcheck.py diff` in the file, and **the assertion was verified to FAIL on injected drift**
(swapping the blocks back turns it red) rather than merely to pass while healthy. Selftest
**100 → 101**. The rule is in CLAUDE.md because the run-13 lesson applies: a correction
written only into `builds/` regresses.

**2. The run-18 prop-baseline rule — refined, sighting 2.** Run 24 pre-registered: *"if a
second occasion arises where every book on a board quotes a store-OUT player whose `ret_date`
falls on or before kickoff, the rule gets a clause."* It arose. **George Kittle (SF, OUT/
Surgery, P(plays)=0.0, `ret_date` 2026-09-10) is quoted by DraftKings +215, Caesars +225 and
FanDuel +240** — and 2026-09-10 **is SF@LA's kickoff day**. The clause now in doctrine: a
`P(plays)=0` whose return date has already arrived is a **CONTESTED availability, not a book
error**; the flag belongs on the store row, not the devig baseline. The run-18 rule stands
unchanged where books *disagree* — that still names a bad book.

**What kept it harmless both times, stated precisely: Kittle appears only in
`player_anytime_td`, which is quoted one-sided (Yes only) and therefore never devigged.** He
contaminates no two-sided baseline. All 51 devigable rungs come from four books.

**One unresolved join, flagged not guessed:** `Deebo Samuel` is quoted on the SF@LA board but
has no 2026 roster row, so no team could be ID-joined and he was excluded from the dog-roster
filter rather than assigned a team from memory. 76 of 90 quoted names resolved; the other 13
exclusions are team D/ST entries, which are not players.

### The board repriced broadly and moved nothing — and the first read of that was WRONG

**66 of 85 matched sides repriced**, 19 held, 28 logged rungs are no longer quoted (retired
lines). **Nothing moved 1.00pp or more; the largest single move is −0.97pp.** Under run 24's
stated ≥1.00pp threshold, **zero supersedes are owed this run.**

| rung | ledger | now | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| NYJ@TEN TEN −3 | 48.80 | 47.83 (+100 @DraftKings) | **−0.97pp** |
| CHI@CAR CHI −3 | 48.80 | 47.83 (+100 @BetUS) | −0.97pp |
| BAL@IND BAL ML | 63.00 | 62.06 (−175 @Bovada) | −0.94pp |
| TB@CIN CIN −3.5 | 49.40 | 50.34 (−108 @BetRivers) | +0.94pp |
| TB@CIN CIN ML | 64.20 | 65.12 (−190 @BetMGM) | +0.92pp |
| GB@MIN MIN −1.5 | 50.00 | 49.08 (−102 @DraftKings) | −0.92pp |

**Recorded because it nearly went into the report:** the first pass of this diff joined
logged rows to the board on `(market, side, point)` and **ignored `game_id`**. Four W1 games
share a 48.5 total and several share a 3.0 spread, so it cross-matched games and produced a
headline **"+2.94pp NO@DET O48.5"** — a move nearly three times the true maximum, on a rung
that had not moved at all. Re-joining through `parse_leg_id()` on the full leg_id gave the
table above. **This is the exact failure class the leg_id codec exists to prevent, committed
by a throwaway analysis script that bypassed the codec** — the ledger tools were never wrong.
The lesson is narrow and worth keeping: **ad-hoc scan scripts must join through `legs.py`,
not through re-assembled column tuples**, or they reintroduce the MLB regex bug class in the
one place nobody is auditing. Registering as sighting 1; if a second run does it, it goes to
doctrine.

A second, smaller process catch: the four new rows were first appended to the END of
`results_log.md`, which is **not** the end of the recommended-legs table — a
`### Played-ticket record (parlays)` subsection sits between it and the BT header, and the
sectioniser resets on any `###`. `calib.py` reported **orphans = 4** and the live count sat
unchanged at 113. **The parser guard added 2026-08-09 caught it exactly as designed**; the
rows were relocated above the subsection and the count went 113 → **117, orphans 0**.

### THE HEADLINE — the first gate-clearing leg, and why it is still NO BET

**Christian McCaffrey Over 4.5 receptions (SF@LA) — +108 @BetRivers — +2.89pp.**

The rung went **two-sided across three books** (DK quotes both sides at 4.5; FanDuel and
BetRivers quote the Over) and the hold **collapsed from +5.95pp at run 24 to +0.23pp** — the
tightest rung of the port, tighter than the featured board's +0.60pp floor. Best Over **+108
@BetRivers** against best Under **−109 @DraftKings** devigs to **no-vig 47.97%** (run 24 read
44.9% off a one-sided 5.95pp rung). SF is the **3.5–4 point dog** by store ID join and the
total is **48.5**, so `script_pass_dog +3pp` fires: **TrueP 50.97% vs breakeven 48.08% =
+2.89pp**, clearing the +2pp standalone bar and falling short of the +3–4pp anchor bar.

**It is NOT a bet, and the reason is the availability gate, not the price gate.**
McCaffrey is **QUESTIONABLE, P(plays)=0.75, ret_date 2026-08-27** — doctrine is explicit that
availability-uncertain legs are **PENDING**, not priced. Logged as PENDING with the clearance
recorded so the next run inherits the number rather than rediscovering it.

**Note what this clearance is NOT.** The run-18 rule says to read a gate clearance as WIDTH
when the baseline is thin. This is the opposite case: three books, the tightest hold on the
board, and an availability cross-check that flags **nobody** (see below). If this leg fails,
it will fail on `script_pass_dog` being a 0-decided-leg directional seed — which is the
honest residual risk and exactly what Week 1 exists to measure.

**Pre-registered re-check condition:** McCaffrey's `ret_date` is **2026-08-27**. If he clears
to no listing (or holds at Q) by the Friday designation run and the rung still reads ≥+2pp,
this is the port's first live candidate. If the hold re-widens past ~+3pp, the clearance was
transient tightness and dies with it.

### Tooling change
`odds_api.sh props` now **caches the raw payload** to `data/.cache/props_<eid>.json` (written
only after the error guard, so an error payload is never cached as a board). The renderer only
printed, so any run wanting to devig the prop board had to re-poll and pay the credits twice —
which is what this run did before fixing it. Future prop scans are free.

**Credits:** started **17571**, ended **17542** — **29 spent** (board served from cache;
four core-prop polls, 16 credits of which were the duplicate re-poll the cache fix now
prevents; 14 absence probes billed only on the two events carrying data, the 12 empty ones
free). **Odds API credits remaining: 17542** (used 2458 this month; shared key).

### THE HEADLINE — the first gate-clearing leg is decaying, and survives on its own terms

**Christian McCaffrey Over 4.5 receptions (SF@LA) — +106 @FanDuel — +2.13pp — still PENDING.**

Run 27's clearance rested on the tightest rung of the port (+0.23pp hold). That tightness was
partly transient: the rung is now **+1.81pp**, an eightfold re-widening, and the best Over
slipped +108 → +106 while the best Under tightened −109 → −114. No-vig falls 47.97% → 47.68%,
breakeven rises 48.08% → 48.54%, and the edge with `script_pass_dog +3pp` goes
**+2.89pp → +2.13pp**.

**Run 27 pre-registered the kill condition — "if the hold re-widens past ~+3pp, the clearance
was transient tightness and dies with it" — and at +1.81pp it is NOT met.** So the leg is
kept, honestly, at a much thinner margin. Both premises re-verified from the store and the
board: SF is still the **3.5-point dog** (modal across 6 books) and the total is still
**48.5**, so `script_pass_dog` still fires.

**It remains PENDING on availability, not on price.** McCaffrey is **QUESTIONABLE,
P(plays)=0.75, ret 2026-08-27** — byte-identical to run 27. The `ret_date` falls **before**
Friday's designation run (2026-08-28 21:00Z), which is where run 27's re-check condition
actually lands. Nothing about that changes here.

What this run adds to the record is a warning about the run-27 framing. Run 27 argued the
clearance was not a WIDTH artefact because the rung was the tightest on the board. Half of
that tightness has now evaporated in 38 hours while the underlying no-vig barely moved
(−0.29pp). **The clearance is therefore more juice-sensitive than run 27 implied**: a further
1pp of hold takes it under the gate without any change in the market's actual opinion. Not yet
a lesson — one sighting — but the re-check on Friday should read the *hold*, not just the edge.

| 5. Bankroll | **no attempt** | balance $10.00, 0/4 consecutive wins, ladder STOP not armed. |

### Availability — 23 findings, and why none of them is a premise

`weekcheck diff` (run POST-sync, per the run-25 rule) returns **exit 1 with 23 findings**
against run 27's snapshot, up from 2. Every one is an availability move; **zero** QB changes,
spread moves ≥1.5, total moves ≥2.0, wind crossings or kickoff changes.

- **5 hard drops (props void class):** Cedrick Wilson Jr. (DET) Q→IR · Keir Thomas II (LA)
  Q→IR · Xavier Weaver (ARI) Q→IR · Devon Marshall (JAX) Q→IR · Trey Moore (MIA) Q→OUT.
- **2 new DOUBTFUL:** Dylan Horton (HOU) · Mason Richman (SEA).
- **16 new QUESTIONABLE**, incl. Jalen Ramsey (PIT), Nnamdi Madubuike (BAL), Theo Johnson
  (NYG), Ben Sinnott (WAS), TreVeyon Henderson (NE), Eli Stowers (PHI).

**None is a premise-bearer for a logged leg.** The four legs on MIA@LV, two on DAL@NYG, one on
WAS@PHI and two on BAL@IND are spreads/totals/ML — no starting QB moved and no line moved, so
those premises stand. The nearest thing to a live concern is **TreVeyon Henderson (NE RB)**,
Q on a game carrying three logged prop legs; he is not an input to any of them (A.J. Brown
receptions, Drake Maye pass TDs), and a Q running back is not a pass-volume premise on its own.
**Ashton Jeanty (LV RB1)** — run 27's one live finding — is unchanged at Q / P=0.75 / ret
08-27, so the MIA@LV re-verification run 27 flagged is still outstanding and still costs
nothing, because nothing is played.

**The snapshot was deliberately NOT refreshed.** `weekcheck snap` is a build-run action; leaving
run 27's snapshot in place means Friday's designation run diffs against the last *build*, which
is what the designation haircut wants to see. Re-snapping here would have silently cleared 23
findings that no leg has yet been re-verified against.

### New process observation — 1st sighting, re-check pre-registered

`weather.py week` labels **NE@SEA "⚠ UNVERIFIED — forecast fetch failed"** at T-15.4d. Probing
Lumen Field directly returns *"outside the returned forecast range"* — i.e. the provider simply
did not reach that far, which is the **HORIZON** condition wearing the **UNVERIFIED** label.
Both branches refuse to apply a weather adjustment, so **nothing is mispriced and no gate
failed**; this is a labeling nit, not a bug. Logging it as a **1st sighting** rather than
fixing it, per the 2-3 sighting bar. **Pre-registered re-check: if a game inside the 16-day
window again reports UNVERIFIED where the provider's range merely falls short, that is sighting
2 and the boundary in `weather.py` gets tightened in CODE** (the run-13 lesson: a correction
written only into `builds/` regresses).
